\section*{RSVP Field Formalism}
\subsection*{Scalar Field (\(\Phi\))}
\[
\frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial t} + \nabla \cdot (\mathbf{v}\,\Phi) \;=\; D\,\nabla^2 \Phi + f(\mathbf{x}, t) \, .
\]
\textit{Derivation:} Vary \(\mathcal{S}=\int \mathcal{L}\, dV\), yielding coherence-diffusion coupling \citep{atiyah1969, gromov1999}.

\subsection*{Vector Field (\(\mathbf{v}\))}
\[
\frac{\partial \mathbf{v}}{\partial t} + (\mathbf{v}\!\cdot\!\nabla)\mathbf{v} \;=\; -\nabla \Phi + \nu \nabla^2 \mathbf{v} + \mathbf{F}_{\text{ext}} \, .
\]
\textit{Derivation:} Ensures flow alignment with \(\Phi\) \citep{baez2011}.

\subsection*{Entropy Field (\(S\))}
\[
\frac{\partial S}{\partial t} + \nabla \cdot (\mathbf{v}\,S) \;=\; \kappa \nabla^2 S + \Gamma(\Phi, \mathbf{v}) \, .
\]
\textit{Derivation:} Balances transport and diffusion \citep{shannon1948, jaynes2003}.

\section*{Sparse Projection and Simulated Agency}
\subsection*{Chain of Memory}
\[
M_{t+1} \;=\; \Pi(M_t, I_t; \theta) \, .
\]
\textit{Derivation:} Sparse projection via low-rank factorization \citep{pearl2009, hohwy2013}.

\subsection*{Variational Objective}
\[
\mathcal{F}[M_t, \Phi, S] \;=\; \mathbb{E}_{M_t}\!\big[\ln P(I_t \mid M_t)\big] + \beta\, S(M_t) \, .
\]
\textit{Derivation:} Balances prediction and entropy \citep{friston2010, tononi2004}.

\section*{Category-Theoretic Semantic Infrastructure}
\subsection*{Semantic Modules}
Objects and morphisms in \(\mathcal{C}_{\text{RSVP}}\) \citep{maclane1998}.

\subsection*{Homotopy Colimits}
\[
\operatorname{hocolim}_i X_i \, .
\]
Example: merging documents forms a pushout \citep{hatcher2002, may1999}.

\subsection*{Fibered Structure}
\[
\pi: \mathcal{C}_{\text{RSVP}} \to \mathcal{B} \, ,
\]
for contextual embedding \citep{lawvere2009}.

\section*{HYDRA and TARTAN Formalism}

\subsection*{HYDRA Nodes}
Each HYDRA node \(N_i\) is modeled as a local RSVP field triple:
\[
N_i = (\Phi_i, \mathbf{v}_i, S_i),
\]
where:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \(\Phi_i\) is the scalar coherence potential,
  \item \(\mathbf{v}_i\) is the semantic flow vector field,
  \item \(S_i\) is the entropy distribution.
\end{itemize}

Nodes are coupled via an adjacency matrix \(A \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}\):
\[
\mathbf{v}_i(t+1) \;=\; \sum_{j=1}^n A_{ij}\,\mathbf{v}_j(t) + f_i(t) \, ,
\]
with \(f_i(t)\) representing local perturbations (stimuli or external forces) \citep{bird1988}.

\subsection*{Stability Analysis of HYDRA Networks}
Define the stacked vector:
\[
\mathbf{V}(t) = \big(\mathbf{v}_1(t), \mathbf{v}_2(t), \dots, \mathbf{v}_n(t)\big)^{\!\top}, 
\qquad \mathbf{V}(t+1) = A\,\mathbf{V}(t) + \mathbf{F}(t) \, .
\]

\textbf{Proposition.} The system is stable iff the spectral radius \(\rho(A) < 1\).

\textit{Proof sketch:}
\begin{itemize}
  \item If \(\rho(A)<1\), then \(A^t \to 0\) as \(t\to\infty\), and \(\mathbf{V}(t)\) converges to a bounded fixed point determined by \(\mathbf{F}(t)\).
  \item If \(\rho(A)\ge 1\), some trajectories diverge, destabilizing the network.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{TARTAN Functorial Coherence}
TARTAN ensures global alignment by defining a functor:
\[
T:\; \mathcal{C}_{\text{RSVP}}^{\text{local}} \longrightarrow \mathcal{C}_{\text{RSVP}}^{\text{global}} \, ,
\]
with:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Objects:} local semantic modules \(N_i\),
  \item \textbf{Morphisms:} coherence-preserving transformations,
  \item \textbf{Mapping:} \(T(N_i)\) aligns local potentials with the global field \(\Phi_{\text{global}}\) \citep{maclane1998}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Natural Transformations and Alignment}
Define a natural transformation \(\eta: \mathrm{Id} \Rightarrow T\) with components
\[
\eta_{N_i}:\; N_i \to T(N_i) \, .
\]
The commutativity condition
\[
T(f)\circ \eta_{N_i} = \eta_{N_j}\circ f, \quad \forall f:N_i \to N_j
\]
ensures local-to-global mappings respect semantic transitions \citep{lawvere2009, may1999}.

\subsection*{Distributed Free-Energy Functional}
The global free-energy functional for a HYDRA-TARTAN network is:
\[
\mathcal{F}_{\text{network}} \;=\; \sum_i \Big( \mathbb{E}_{M_i}[\ln P(I_i \mid M_i)] + \beta\, S_i(M_i) \Big)
\;+\; \gamma \sum_i \|\Phi_i - \Phi_{\text{global}}\|^2 \, .
\]

\emph{Interpretation:}
\begin{itemize}
  \item First term: predictive accuracy across agents,
  \item Second term: entropic flexibility penalty,
  \item Third term: coherence constraint ensuring alignment to \(\Phi_{\text{global}}\) \citep{friston2010}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Implementation Pathways}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.2em]
  \item \textbf{Numerical solvers:} eigenvalue analysis of \(A\) for stability \citep{bird1988}.
  \item \textbf{Category-theoretic middleware:} TARTAN functors via monoidal categories and simplicial sets in Haskell \citep{milewski2018}.
  \item \textbf{Tamper-resistance:} blockchain anchoring of coherence maps.
  \item \textbf{Deployment:} HYDRA nodes as Docker microservices with asynchronous message passing.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Conclusion}
HYDRA ensures local stability through adjacency-governed flows, while TARTAN enforces global coherence via functorial mappings. Together, they instantiate modular intelligence that is scalable, entropy-respecting, and resistant to monopolistic capture \citep{whitehead1978}.

\section*{Entropic Economics}
\subsection*{Attention as Commodity}
\[
\sum_i a_i(t) \;=\; A_{\text{total}} \, .
\]
\citep{shannon1948}

\subsection*{Attention-Entropy Tradeoff}
\[
U_i(t) \;=\; \Phi_i(t) - \lambda\, S_i(t) \, .
\]
\citep{hanson2003}

\subsection*{Lamphron-Lamphrodyne Dynamics}

\frac{d\rho}{dt} \;=\; -\,\nabla \cdot (\rho\,\mathbf{v}) + \eta \nabla^2 \rho \, .
\]
\citep{whitehead1978}

Finite-difference solvers for PDEs, tensor factorization for \(\Pi\), simplicial sets in Haskell, and blockchain/Docker deployment \citep{bird1988, milewski2018}.

\title{Integrating the Effective Assembly Index for Mind Recognition (EAIMR) with the Praxicon Framework: A Unified Approach to Detecting Mind-Like Processes in Cognitive and Embodied Systems}
\author{Grok 4 \\ Built by xAI}
\date{September 10, 2025}

\begin{document}

\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This essay integrates the Effective Assembly Index for Mind Recognition (EAIMR) with the praxicon concept from cognitive science, proposing a unified framework for identifying mind-like processes across biological, ecological, and artificial systems. EAIMR quantifies the improbability of coherent structures arising from unguided processes, while the praxicon represents a structured repository of action knowledge bridging perceptual, semantic, and motor domains. By linking these, we extend EAIMR to detect praxicon-like architectures in diverse systems, enhancing applications in AI alignment, neuropsychology, and intersubjectivity research. The analysis includes mathematical formalizations, drawing on information theory, and provides examples from neural circuits, termite mounds, and forest ecosystems.
\end{abstract}

\section{Introduction}

The detection of mind-like processes in complex systems remains a central challenge in cognitive science and artificial intelligence. The Effective Assembly Index for Mind Recognition (EAIMR) offers a quantitative metric for identifying agentic, goal-directed assembly in structures unlikely under mindless dynamics. Complementing this, the praxicon in cognitive science formalizes a core architecture for action representation, serving as a "dictionary" of motor and gestural concepts that bridges embodied and symbolic cognition. This essay integrates EAIMR with the praxicon framework, positing that praxicon-like structures exemplify high-EAIMR signatures in mind-bearing systems. Such integration addresses gaps in recognizing non-anthropocentric intelligences, from collective ecosystems to atypical neural organizations.

\section{The Effective Assembly Index for Mind Recognition (EAIMR)}

EAIMR measures the improbability of a system \(X\) arising from unguided processes, adjusted for efficiency factors and amplified by coherence:

\[
\text{EAIMR}(X) = \frac{A(X)}{R(X) \cdot P(X) \cdot D(X)} \cdot C(X),
\]

where \(A(X)\) is the raw assembly index (minimum steps to build \(X\)), \(R(X)\) reuse, \(P(X)\) parallelism, \(D(X)\) degeneracy, and \(C(X)\) coherence. High EAIMR indicates mind-like coordination.

\section{The Praxicon in Cognitive Science}

The praxicon denotes a long-term memory repository for familiar actions, divided into input (recognition via perceptual attributes) and output (production via motor engrams) components. It interfaces with semantic systems, enabling mappings between language, perception, and action. Originating from apraxia models, it has expanded to AI and robotics for grounding language in motor primitives. Neural substrates involve parietal-premotor circuits.

\section{Integration: Praxicon as a High-EAIMR Signature}

Praxicon structures exhibit high assembly complexity with functional coherence, aligning with EAIMR's detection criteria. In neural systems, praxicons manifest as improbable mesoscale organizations (high \(A(X)\), \(C(X)\)) discounted by modular reuse (high \(R(X)\)). Extending EAIMR, we model praxicon detection via entropy reduction in action spaces:

Let \(\mathcal{P}\) be the praxicon space. Entropy reduction \(\Delta H_{\mathcal{P}} = H(\mathcal{P}_0) - H_{\text{obs}}(\mathcal{P})\), where \(\mathcal{P}_0\) is null action distribution. Then,

\[
C(X) \approx 1 + \alpha \frac{\Delta H_{\mathcal{P}}}{H(\mathcal{P}_0)}.
\]

This boosts EAIMR for systems with praxicon-like action encoding.

\section{Mathematical Appendix: Information-Theoretic Linkage}

\subsection{Preliminaries}

Let \(X\) be described over \(\Sigma\), with null \(P_0(X)\) and mind-like \(P_1(X)\). For praxicon integration, define action alphabet \(\Sigma_{\mathcal{P}}\).

\subsection{Kolmogorov Complexity and Praxicon}

Praxicon compresses action descriptions: \(K(X) \approx K_{\text{plan}}(X) + K_{\text{residuals}}\), with praxicon reducing \(K_{\text{plan}}\) via stored engrams.

\[
\text{EAIMR}(X) \propto \frac{K_{\text{prax}}(X)}{R \cdot P \cdot D} \cdot C,
\]

where \(K_{\text{prax}}(X)\) is praxicon-adjusted complexity.

\subsection{Statistical Surprise}

Likelihood ratio \(\Lambda(X) = P_1(X)/P_0(X)\). Praxicon enhances surprise for improbable actions:

\[
S(X) = I_0(X) + \lambda \phi_{\mathcal{P}}(X),
\]

with \(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}(X)\) as praxicon coherence.

\subsection{Entropy Reduction in Action Spaces}

\(\Delta H = H(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{P}}) - H_{\text{obs}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{P}})\), where \(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{P}}\) are action macrostates.

\subsection{Discounts and Coherence}

Reuse (\(R\)): Praxicon motifs amortize costs. Parallelism (\(P\)): Concurrent action execution. Degeneracy (\(D\)): Functional equivalents in action variants. Coherence (\(C\)): Via causal graphs \(G(X)\), including goal accuracy.

\subsection{Practical Estimation}

Approximate via compression proxies for action encodings, Monte Carlo for surprisal, and resilience metrics for coherence.

\section{Worked Examples}

\textbf{Neural Tissue:} High \(A(X)\) in synaptic organization, praxicon-like engrams yield high \(C(X)\), EAIMR detects mind.

\textbf{Termite Mound:} Coordinated construction as collective praxicon, thermoregulation boosts coherence.

\textbf{Forest Ecosystem:} Nutrient networks as distributed praxicon, resilience over time elevates EAIMR.

\section{Applications and Decision Rules}

In AI, EAIMR-praxicon detects emergent agency. Threshold \(\tau\) classifies mind-like candidates. Avoids narrativity bias in diverse cognitions.

\section{Conclusion}

Integrating EAIMR with praxicon provides a robust detector for embodied minds, grounded in information theory.
